Um, yeah, I think... I hope I'm explaining this okay. It's kind of both, maybe? I really don't want to sound lazy, I promise I'm usually not like this at all.

Um... no, I wouldn't say I've *completely* lost interest. I mean, I still like the idea of doing those things! I really do. I still want to be the kind of person who goes hiking and tries new recipes.

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus, make decisions, or think clearly compared to how you used to feel?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe just a little bit? I don't want to sound like I'm completely disorganized or anything, because I really do try my best to keep on top of things. But... I guess if I'm being thorough for you, I have noticed that when I try to work on, like, my catering forms or this Chamber application I've been looking at, I end up reading the same line over and over again. \n\nAnd then I get kind of stuck on really small choices? Like, um, what price to put down, or even just figuring out which email I should answer first. It's silly, I know. I usually have a really good head on my shoulders! People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, it's not that I don't get *any* pleasure from them. I don't want it to sound like I'm totally miserable or anything. But... I guess if I'm being completely honest, which I know I'm supposed to be for you... it is a lot less than it used to be. 

Like, I came in today because I realized I just barely care about the things that used to make me feel like myself. Dance class, hiking, trying new recipes... even game night with my friends. They just kind of sit there now, almost like chores that I keep putting off. I still go through the motions sometimes because I want to be a good sport, but the spark just isn't really there right now. I hope that doesn't make me sound awful or ungrateful for the good things in my life. Do you think that's normal when someone's been under a lot of stress? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture.

Dance class, hiking, trying new recipes... even game night with my friends. They just kind of sit there now, almost like chores that I keep putting off. I still go through the motions sometimes because I want to be a good sport, but the spark just isn't really there right now. I hope that doesn't make me sound awful or ungrateful for the good things in my life. Do you think that's normal when someone's been under a lot of stress? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve noticed that almost all of your usual activities, not just a few, don’t bring you any enjoyment anymore, even when you do them?\n\nPatient: Um... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... actually, I want to be really careful not to exaggerate, because I know you need the exact facts to help me. It hasn't been every single day, or even most of the day.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... I'm sorry, I think that might be the same question you just asked? I really hope I didn't confuse you or answer it wrong a second ago! I'm trying my best to be clear, I promise.

Um... well, I guess... yes, actually. I'm sorry, I know I just kept saying no to the two-week thing, but... this part is actually why I came in. I hope it's okay to admit that.

I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, for the past couple of weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I mean, I have my moments where I feel a little flat, I guess, but definitely not all day, every day. \n\nI try really hard to keep a positive attitude, you know? I don't want to be a burden on anyone by being down all the time. So, no, I don't think I'd call it depressed. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling more tired or lacking energy lately, even when you haven’t done anything physically exhausting?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I sleep pretty well, usually, so I wouldn't say I'm overly tired or anything. I try to make sure I get enough rest so I can keep up with things. \n\nThere are days where I might feel a little sluggish, but I think that's normal, right? Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making things confusing.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing less than usual during the day, or having trouble getting started with even small tasks, like chores or work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I'm doing less. I really try to keep on top of things around the house, you know? I don't want things to pile up or for anyone to think I'm just letting everything go. \n\nI mean, maybe sometimes it takes me a little longer to get started on the laundry or the dishes, but I always get them done. I just really want to show that I'm trying my best.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about small things, or maybe feeling like you’re not good enough or worthless, even when there’s no real reason for it?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I feel worthless. I really try not to let my mind go there, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm completely disorganized or anything, because I really do try my best to keep on top of things. But... I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm just making excuses.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there have been days when you felt down or in a bit of a low mood for most of the day, even if it wasn’t all-consuming or severe?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say I've felt down or in a low mood for most of the day.
